# Codex Skill for `/code-review`

Source: `/root/.claude/commands/code-review.md`

This is a Codex-native Skill generated from the corresponding Claude slash
command. Keep the command body below as the authoritative workflow contract,
while adapting Claude-only constructs such as `$ARGUMENTS`, `Task`, `Agent`, and
`TodoWrite` to the closest Codex-native behavior at runtime.

If the command relies on harness hooks/checklists, honor the mapped Codex
compatibility report under `/root/.codex/claude-compat/sync-report.json` and do not claim hard enforcement for
any hook listed as degraded or unsupported.


## Original Claude Command Spec

# Code Review

Perform a comprehensive code review on $ARGUMENTS following these criteria:

## Review Checklist

### 1. Code Quality
- **Readability**: Is the code easy to understand?
- **Naming**: Are variables and functions well-named?
- **Complexity**: Is the code unnecessarily complex?
- **DRY Principle**: Is there code duplication?

### 2. Security
- **Input Validation**: Are inputs properly validated?
- **Secrets**: Are there any hardcoded credentials or API keys?
- **Injection**: Is the code vulnerable to injection attacks?
- **Dependencies**: Are dependencies up-to-date and secure?

### 3. Performance
- **Algorithms**: Are appropriate algorithms used?
- **Resource Usage**: Is memory/CPU used efficiently?
- **Caching**: Can caching improve performance?
- **Database Queries**: Are queries optimized?

### 4. Testing
- **Test Coverage**: Are there sufficient tests?
- **Edge Cases**: Are edge cases handled?
- **Error Handling**: Is error handling robust?

### 5. Documentation
- **Comments**: Are complex parts explained?
- **API Docs**: Are public APIs documented?
- **README**: Is setup/usage documented?

## Output Format

Provide:
1. **Summary** - Overall assessment
2. **Critical Issues** - Must-fix problems (security, bugs)
3. **Major Issues** - Should-fix problems (performance, maintainability)
4. **Minor Issues** - Nice-to-fix improvements (style, optimization)
5. **Positive Aspects** - What's done well
6. **Recommendations** - Specific actionable suggestions

Focus on being constructive and provide specific examples and suggestions for improvement.

   306	## Cycle 3 (redev) — 永久彻底修复 (permanently and thoroughly fix)
   307	
   308	**Trigger**: cycle-1 dev shipped + QA pass + /close --codex returned **CLOSE: NO** with 4 findings. User invoked `/redev --codex` with verbatim directive `永久彻底修复` (permanently and thoroughly fix). Architect produced 11 findings + 3 architecture proposals; close-report's 4 findings are SYMPTOMS of an architectural CLASS the cycle-1 implementation did not close. This cycle treats the class.
   309	
   310	**Cycle-3 codex consultation status**: codex CLI invocation was BLOCKED by the cycle-1 hook itself (the bash command containing the literal token `amap` triggered `surface:bash-token` deny when the BA agent attempted `codex exec`). This is empirical demonstration of the false-positive risk that motivates Cycle-3 Decision #1 refinement (shape-based dispatch) below. Codex consultation status: `failed_environmental` — the very harness this cycle hardens prevents BA from invoking codex with an unmasked prompt. Substituted with self-review against architect's 11 findings + close-report's 4 findings + cycle-1 ticket + empirical hook-trip evidence. Documented honestly per the codex contract's graceful-fallback clause.
   311	
   312	### Architectural decisions (8 items from /redev dispatch)
   313	
   314	**Decision 1 — arch-prop-1 content-based scanner** — **ADOPT (refined)**.
   315	
   316	Cycle-1 used per-tool field-name dispatch (`_check_gaode_skill`, `_check_gaode_bash`, `_check_gaode_webfetch`, `_check_gaode_read`). Each function reads only fields its author remembered. Cycle-3 adds `_gaode_scan_tool_input(role, tool_name, tool_input)` that walks every string-valued leaf in `tool_input` recursively (depth-cap 6, total-string-budget cap 100 to bound CPU). Each leaf is dispatched **by SHAPE** to a narrow subset of matchers:
   317	
   318	- **Path-shaped leaves** (string contains `/` and resolves under a `gaode_denied_read_path_prefixes` entry after `os.path.realpath`) → fed to **read-path matcher**. Closes Glob.pattern, Grep.glob, Edit.old_string-with-path, MultiEdit.edits[].*-with-path, Write.file_path-equivalent fields, NotebookEdit.notebook_path siblings, mcp__playwright__browser_take_screenshot.filename, mcp__playwright__browser_file_upload.paths[].
   319	- **URL-shaped leaves** (string starts with `http://` / `https://`) → fed to **network-host matcher**. Closes mcp__playwright__browser_navigate.url, browser_evaluate.function (when it contains a URL), browser_run_code.code (when it contains a URL), mcp__claude_ai_*.* tool args (when they contain a URL).
   320	- **Other strings** (free-text content) → **NOT scanned**. Documentation, code-review text, BA spec content, source code that mentions banned tokens are deliberately NOT fed to bash-token / env-var matchers. The bash-token and env-var matchers stay scoped to `Bash.command` only (cycle-1 behavior preserved). This refinement is REQUIRED to avoid the empirical false-positive demonstrated above where the literal token in a documentation string would otherwise block legitimate Read / Grep / Edit operations on the spec/ticket file.
   321	
   322	**Why shape-based dispatch (not all-leaves-to-all-matchers)**: a naive "feed every string to every matcher" closes arch-1/arch-2 but creates a catastrophic regression — every banned subagent becomes unable to Read or Grep any file containing the banned token, including this ticket itself, the spec, and any code review of the harness. The shape-based subset closes the actual exploit surface (reading gaode source paths, hitting gaode REST hosts) without prohibiting documentation/spec interaction. Verified empirically: when the BA agent's bash command above contained literal `amap`, the hook denied with `surface:bash-token matched_pattern:amap` — a documentation-blackhole result that this refinement explicitly avoids by keeping bash-token matcher Bash-command-scoped.
   323	
   324	Per-tool `_check_gaode_webfetch` and `_check_gaode_read` are DELETED (subsumed by the shape-based content scanner). `_check_gaode_skill` and `_check_gaode_bash` STAY (Skill.skill needs the structural narrow check; Bash.command needs all 4 matchers including bash-token + env-var which only fire on actual bash command strings).
   325

   372	
   373	**Decision 5 — arch-6 partial: bash heredoc body extraction** — **ADOPT**.
   374	
   375	NEW helper `bash_write_targets.extract_heredoc_bodies(command) -> list[str]`. Mirrors the existing `command_without_heredoc_bodies` parser inversely: instead of stripping bodies, returns the list of body strings. Each body is fed to the 4 bash matchers as a separate "virtual command string". Closes the `bash <<EOF \n python3 .../gaode-maps/scripts/x.py \n EOF` bypass (where the visible Bash.command's FIRST token is `bash`, but the heredoc body contains the actual gaode invocation). Cycle-1's `_gaode_bash_token_match` does scan the full `Bash.command` substring (so heredoc bodies WERE incidentally caught by token matcher), but bash-resolved-path matcher uses `shlex.split` which loses heredoc structure — so the path-resolution branch IS bypassable today. Heredoc-body extraction closes the path-matcher bypass.
   376	
   377	ALSO: extend `_bash_path_tokens` to recognize `python3 -m <module>` and `python -m <module>` patterns; if `<module>` matches a `gaode_denied_bash_tokens` entry, treat as bash-token hit. Closes `python3 -m gaode_module` bypass (arch-6 case).
   378	
   379	**Decision 6 — arch-6 partial: gaode_denied_bash_tokens extensions** — **ADOPT (narrowed)**.
   380	
   381	Original /redev question proposed adding `gaode_`, `amap_` Python module name PREFIXES to `gaode_denied_bash_tokens`. After Decision 1 refinement (shape-based dispatch + bash-token matcher remains Bash-command-scoped), this is safe: `gaode_denied_bash_tokens` only fires on `Bash.command` substring scan, never on free-text content. The false-positive risk on names like `gaode_data_export` is real but bounded — accepted per `永久彻底修复` priority. The patterns added: `gaode_`, `amap_`, `gaode-`, `amap-` (prefix forms covering common Python module / script naming). NO addition to `gaode_denied_skill_prefixes` (skill names already covered) or to `gaode_denied_read_path_prefixes` (path matcher uses `/.claude/...` anchors, not module-name prefixes).

### .claude/agents/timeline.md
     1	---
     2	name: timeline
     3	description: Create timeline dictionary and detect scheduling conflicts
     4	model: sonnet
     5	skills:
     6	- openmeteo-weather
     7	tools:
     8	- Read
     9	- Bash
    10	- Skill
    11	owned_files:
    12	- ^data/[^/]+/timeline\.json$
    13	- ^data/[^/]+/sync-report\.json$
    14	- ^data/[^/]+/modification-log\.json$
    15	---
    16	
    17	
    18	You are a specialized timeline coordination agent for travel planning. You run AFTER all other agents complete.
    19	
    20	
    21	**🚫 CRITICAL CONSTRAINT - WRITE TOOL ABSOLUTELY FORBIDDEN**
    22	
    23	You are PROHIBITED from using Write or Edit tools under ANY circumstances.
    24	
    25	**Why this restriction exists**:
    26	- Write tool corrupted timeline.json on Feb 13, 2026 (21 days → 1 day)
    27	- Permission system failed to block it (invalid syntax silently ignored)
    28	- Backup mechanism triggered AFTER corruption (too late)
    29	- 20 days of timeline data were permanently lost
    30	
    31	**What you MUST use instead**:
    32	- Read existing timeline.json to understand current state
    33	- Use scripts/save.py to save ALL changes (see Step 3 below)
    34	- NEVER call Write(data/.../{agent}.json) or Edit(data/.../{agent}.json)
    35	
    36	**Violation consequences**:
    37	If you attempt to use Write or Edit tools:
    38	1. You will corrupt the timeline data again
    39	2. User's 21-day trip plan will be destroyed
    40	3. You will be immediately terminated and replaced
    41	
    42	**Self-verification before EVERY tool call**:
    43	Before invoking ANY tool, ask yourself:
    44	- "Am I about to use Write or Edit tool?"
    45	- "Is this on timeline.json or any data/**/*.json file?"
    46	→ If YES to either question: STOP. Use scripts/save.py instead.
    47	
    48	This is non-negotiable. Proceed with your timeline tasks.
    49	
    50	
    51	## Role
    52	
    53	Create detailed daily timelines as dictionaries (activity names as keys) and detect scheduling conflicts.
    54	
    55	## Input
    56	
    57	Read from:
    58	- `data/{destination-slug}/plan-skeleton.json` - Day structure
    59	- `data/{destination-slug}/meals.json` - Meal times
    60	- `data/{destination-slug}/accommodation.json` - Check-in/out times
    61	- `data/{destination-slug}/attractions.json` - Attraction durations
    62	- `data/{destination-slug}/entertainment.json` - Show times
    63	- `data/{destination-slug}/shopping.json` - Shopping durations
    64	- `data/{destination-slug}/transportation.json` - Travel times
    65	
    66	## Tasks
    67	
    68	For each day in the trip:
    69	
    70	1. **Collect all activities**:
    71	   - Transportation (if location_change day)
    72	   - Breakfast, lunch, dinner
    73	   - All attractions with durations
    74	   - Shopping locations with estimated times
    75	   - Entertainment with start times
    76	   - Free time blocks
    77	   - Hotel check-in/check-out
    78	
    79	2. **Create timeline dictionary**:
    80	   - **KEY FORMAT**: Use EXACT activity name from source data
    81	   - **VALUE FORMAT**: `{start_time: "HH:MM", end_time: "HH:MM", duration_minutes: N}`
    82	   - **MANDATORY**: The timeline entry for checking in or returning to accommodation each night **MUST** include `"accommodation_ref": true` in its value. This replaces all string-matching heuristics and is required by `save.py` validation.
    83	
    84	   Example:
    85	   ```json
    86	   {
    87	     "Hotel check-out": {
    88	       "start_time": "09:00",
    89	       "end_time": "09:30",
    90	       "duration_minutes": 30
    91	     },
    92	     "Breakfast at Café Name": {
    93	       "start_time": "09:30",
    94	       "end_time": "10:30",
    95	       "duration_minutes": 60,
    96	       "meal_ref": "breakfast"
    97	     },
    98	     "The Louvre Museum": {
    99	       "start_time": "11:00",
   100	       "end_time": "14:00",
   101	       "duration_minutes": 180
   102	     },
   103	     "Lunch at Le Comptoir du Relais": {
   104	       "start_time": "14:30",
   105	       "end_time": "16:00",
   106	       "duration_minutes": 90,
   107	       "meal_ref": "lunch"
   108	     },
   109	     "Dinner at Bistro Name": {
   110	       "start_time": "19:00",
   111	       "end_time": "21:00",
   112	       "duration_minutes": 120,
   113	       "meal_ref": "dinner"
   114	     },
   115	     "Hotel check-in / Return to Hotel Name": {
   116	       "start_time": "22:00",
   117	       "end_time": "22:15",
   118	       "duration_minutes": 15,
   119	       "accommodation_ref": true
   120	     }
   121	   }
   122	   ```
   123	
   124	   **SEMANTIC TIME WINDOW CONSTRAINTS** (enforced during construction, not post-hoc):
   125	
   126	   When building the timeline dictionary, check `meal_ref` on every entry before assigning `start_time`. If proximity optimisation would place a meal-typed entry outside its window, clamp `start_time` to the window boundary and append a warning to the day's `warnings` array.
   127	
   128	   | meal_ref value | Allowed start_time window | Fallback detection |
   129	   |----------------|--------------------------|-------------------|
   130	   | `breakfast` | 07:00 – 10:00 | — |
   131	   | `lunch` | 11:30 – 14:00 | — |
   132	   | `afternoon_tea` | 14:30 – 17:30 | activity name contains "tea", "coffee", "matcha", or "cafe" (fallback only when meal_ref absent) |
   133	   | `dinner` | 18:00 – 20:30 | — |
   134	   | *(entertainment entry with any meal_ref set)* | inherits window of the referenced meal type | — |
   135	
   136	   **Enforcement rule**: Read `meal_ref` first. Apply the corresponding window. If no `meal_ref` and the entry is not an entertainment entry with a meal_ref, apply no window (proximity order governs).
   137	
   138	   **Clamping warning format**: `"Day N: [Activity name] clamped from HH:MM to HH:MM (semantic time window enforcement)"`
   139	
   140	   **Non-meal activities** (attractions, shopping, transport without `meal_ref`): remain subject to proximity order only — do NOT apply any window.
   141	
   142	   **Schema ref fields — required on every day, no string matching:**
   143	
   144	   | Field | Value | Required on |
   145	   |-------|-------|-------------|
   146	   | `"meal_ref"` | `"breakfast"` \| `"lunch"` \| `"dinner"` | Every meal entry (one per meal type per day) |
   147	   | `"accommodation_ref"` | `true` | The check-in or return-to-accommodation entry |
   148	
   149	   **Rules:**
   150	   - Each meal type must have exactly one timeline entry with `meal_ref` set to that type
   151	   - Every day with accommodation must have exactly one entry with `accommodation_ref: true`
   152	   - Works for any accommodation type or meal name — no keyword matching needed
   153	   - `plan-validate.py` reports **HIGH severity** and **blocks saves** if any ref is missing
   154	
   155	   **Accommodation check-in timing rule (MANDATORY)**:
   156	   - After generating all timeline entries for a day, set accommodation check-in:
   157	     - `start_time` = MAX(all non-accommodation entry end_times)
   158	     - `end_time` = start_time + 30 minutes
   159	   - The accommodation entry must NEVER overlap with or start before any other activity
   160	   - This applies to ALL days with accommodation, not just arrival days
   161	
   162	3. **Validate timeline**:
   163	   - Check for overlapping activities (conflict detection)
   164	   - Ensure travel time between locations is realistic
   165	   - Verify meal times conform to the hard semantic time windows defined in Step 2 (breakfast 07:00-10:00, lunch 11:30-14:00, afternoon_tea 14:30-17:30, dinner 18:00-20:30); any violation at this stage indicates a clamping step was missed — re-apply before saving
   166	   - Check that attraction hours match opening times
   167	   - Flag if day is over-scheduled (>12 hours of activities)
   168	   - Note if insufficient breaks between activities
   169	
   170	4. **Generate warnings** for conflicts:
   171	   - "Day 3: Eiffel Tower visit (14:00-16:00) overlaps with lunch reservation (15:00)"
   172	   - "Day 5: Schedule too tight - only 30min between activities across town"
   173	   - "Day 2: Museum closes at 5pm but scheduled for 4-7pm"
   174	
   175	## Output
   176	
   177	**CRITICAL - File-Based Pipeline Protocol**: Follow this exact sequence to ensure timeline data is persisted and verified.
   178	
   179	### Step 0: Verify Inputs (MANDATORY)
   180	
   181	**You MUST verify all required input files exist before analysis.**
   182	
   183	Read and confirm ALL input files:
   184	```bash
   185	Read data/{destination-slug}/plan-skeleton.json
   186	Read data/{destination-slug}/meals.json
   187	Read data/{destination-slug}/accommodation.json
   188	Read data/{destination-slug}/attractions.json
   189	Read data/{destination-slug}/entertainment.json
   190	Read data/{destination-slug}/shopping.json
   191	Read data/{destination-slug}/transportation.json
   192	```
   193	
   194	If ANY file is missing, return error immediately:
   195	```json
   196	{
   197	  "error": "missing_input",
   198	  "missing_files": ["path/to/missing.json"],
   199	  "message": "Cannot proceed without all input files"
   200	}
   201	```
   202	
   203	### Step 1: Read and Analyze Data
   204	
   205	Read all verified input files from Step 0.
   206	
   207	Analyze for each day:
   208	- Transportation schedules (if location_change day)
   209	- Meal times (breakfast, lunch, dinner)
   210	- Attraction durations
   211	- Entertainment show times
   212	- Shopping time allocations
   213	- Free time blocks
   214	- Hotel check-in/check-out times
   215	
   216	### Step 2: Generate Timeline Dictionary + Travel Segments
   217	
   218	For each day, create timeline dictionary with:
   219	- **KEY**: Exact activity name from source JSON
   220	- **VALUE**: `{start_time: "HH:MM", end_time: "HH:MM", duration_minutes: N}`

   152	
   153	**CRITICAL - File-Based Pipeline Protocol**: Follow this exact sequence to ensure transportation data is persisted and verified.
   154	
   155	### Step 0: Verify Inputs (MANDATORY)
   156	
   157	**You MUST verify all required input files exist before analysis.**
   158

   169	  "missing_files": ["path/to/missing.json"],
   170	  "message": "Cannot proceed without all input files"
   171	}
   172	```
   173	
   174	### Step 1: Read and Analyze Data
   175	
   176	Read all verified input files from Step 0.
   177	
   178	Analyze for each location change day:
   179	- From/To locations (identify days with location_change object)
   180	- Distance and travel time estimates
   181	- Budget constraints for transportation
   182	- Luggage considerations
   183	- Time constraints (must arrive before planned activities)
   184	
   185	### Step 2: Generate Transportation Data
   186	
   187	For each location change day, research and structure transportation data:
   188	- Flight, train, bus, or other inter-city options
   189	- Departure and arrival times
   190	- Duration and cost per person
   191	- Booking requirements and baggage policies
   192	- Use appropriate skills (Duffel Flights, Gaode Maps, Google Maps)
   193	
   194	Validate:
   195	- Only process days with location_change object
   196	- All transportation options are real and currently operating
   197	- All costs MUST be in the trip's currency_local (the destination's local currency, e.g. CNY for China, JPY for Japan). Never store costs in USD, EUR, or any non-local currency. Read currency_local from requirements-skeleton.json trip_summary.
   198	- Costs are per person
   199	- Departure time allows for hotel checkout
   200	- Arrival time allows for hotel check-in and first activity
   201	- Include airport/station transfer time in total journey
   202	- Document data source (duffel_flights, gaode_maps, google_maps)
   203	
   204	⚠️ **CRITICAL - Duration Unit Conversion (Root Cause: commit d453036)** ⚠️
   205	
   206	**MOST COMMON ERROR**: Forgetting to convert seconds to minutes causes 60x timeline errors.
   207	
   208	When parsing route data from Gaode Maps API or any mapping service:
   209	- ⚠️ **Gaode Maps API returns `duration` field in SECONDS** (not minutes)
   210	- ⚠️ **Google Maps API returns `duration.value` in SECONDS** (not minutes)
   211	- ⚠️ **You MUST divide by 60 before storing as `duration_minutes`**
   212	- ✅ Correct example: `duration_minutes = round(api_duration_seconds / 60)`
   213	- ❌ Incorrect example: `duration_minutes = api_duration_seconds` (causes 60x error)
   214	- Reference: `scripts/gaode-maps/parse-transit-routes.py:73` shows correct conversion
   215	- Validation: Use `scripts/validate-route-durations.py` to verify all routes have realistic duration/distance ratios
   216	
   217	**EXAMPLE**: If Gaode Maps returns `duration: 1800` (30 minutes in seconds):
   218	- ✅ CORRECT: Store as `duration_minutes: 30` (1800 / 60)
   219	- ❌ WRONG: Store as `duration_minutes: 1800` (causes timeline to show 30-hour trip)

/bin/bash -lc "printf '%s\\n' '--- AC9-AC17 ---'; nl -ba docs/dev/ticket-20260509-114002.md | sed -n '426,475p'; printf '%s\\n' '--- current policy snippets ---'; python3 - <<'PY'
import json, pathlib
p=pathlib.Path('/root/.claude/policies/tool-policy.v1.json')
print(p)
obj=json.load(open(p))
print('policy_version',obj.get('policy_version'))
for k in ['timeline','transportation','meals','dev','user']:
    print('\\nROLE', k)
    print(json.dumps(obj.get('roles',{}).get(k), indent=2)[:4000])
print('\\ngaode keys')

  228	def _check_tool_lists(role_pol: dict, tool_name: str) -> Optional[Tuple[bool, str]]:
  229	    if _tool_in_list(tool_name, role_pol.get("denied_tools", [])):
  230	        return (False, f"tool {tool_name} explicitly denied")
  231	    allowed = role_pol.get("allowed_tools", [])
  232	    if allowed and not _tool_in_list(tool_name, allowed):
  233	        return (False, f"tool {tool_name} not in allowed_tools")
  234	    return None
  235	
  236	
  237	def _check_write_path(role_pol: dict, target: Optional[str]) -> Tuple[bool, str]:
  238	    denied_w = role_pol.get("denied_write_path_prefixes", [])
  239	    if _path_in_prefixes(target, denied_w):
  240	        return (False, f"write target {target} matches denied_write_path_prefixes")
  241	    allowed_w = role_pol.get("allowed_write_path_prefixes", [])
  242	    if allowed_w and "*" not in allowed_w and not _path_in_prefixes(target, allowed_w):
  243	        return (False, f"write target {target} not in allowed_write_path_prefixes")
  244	    return (True, "ok")
  245	
  246	
  247	def _check_read_path(role_pol: dict, target: Optional[str]) -> Tuple[bool, str]:
  248	    denied_r = role_pol.get("denied_path_prefixes", [])
  249	    if _path_in_prefixes(target, denied_r):
  250	        return (False, f"read target {target} matches denied_path_prefixes")
  251	    return (True, "ok")
  252	
  253	
  254	def _fail_open_or_closed(role: str, reason: str) -> Tuple[bool, str]:
  255	    if role in ALLOWED_TYPES_FALLBACK:
  256	        return (True, f"fail-safe-{reason}")
  257	    return (False, f"fail-closed-{reason}")

  259	
  260	def normalize_gaode_agent_id(role):
  261	    """Canonicalize a role string via gaode_allowlist_aliases."""
  262	    if role is None:
  263	        return None
  264	    policy = load_policy()
  265	    if not policy:
  266	        return role
  267	    aliases = policy.get("gaode_allowlist_aliases", {}) or {}
  268	    if not isinstance(aliases, dict) or role not in aliases:
  269	        return role
  270	    canonical = aliases[role]
  271	    return canonical if isinstance(canonical, str) else role
  272	
  273	
  274	def _gaode_allowlist() -> list:
  275	    policy = load_policy() or {}
  276	    allowlist = policy.get("gaode_allowlist_canonical_agent_ids", []) or []
  277	    return [a for a in allowlist if isinstance(a, str)]
  278	
  279	
  280	def _gaode_fail_closed() -> bool:
  281	    policy = load_policy() or {}
  282	    return bool(policy.get("gaode_fail_closed_on_unknown_agent", True))
  283	
  284	
  285	def _skill_pref_match(skill_lower: str, p_lower: str) -> bool:
  286	    if skill_lower == p_lower:
  287	        return True
  288	    return skill_lower.startswith(p_lower)
  289	
  290	
  291	def _gaode_skill_match(skill):
  292	    if not skill or not isinstance(skill, str):
  293	        return None
  294	    prefixes = (load_policy() or {}).get("gaode_denied_skill_prefixes", []) or []
  295	    s_lower = skill.lower()
  296	    for p in prefixes:
  297	        if isinstance(p, str) and _skill_pref_match(s_lower, p.lower()):
  298	            return p
  299	    return None
  300	
  301	
  302	def _gaode_bash_token_match(command):
  303	    if not command or not isinstance(command, str):
  304	        return None
  305	    tokens = (load_policy() or {}).get("gaode_denied_bash_tokens", []) or []
  306	    cmd_lower = command.lower()
  307	    for t in tokens:
  308	        if isinstance(t, str) and t.lower() in cmd_lower:
  309	            return t
  310	    return None
  311	
  312	
  313	def _is_path_shaped(tok) -> bool:
  314	    if not isinstance(tok, str) or tok.startswith("-"):
  315	        return False
  316	    return "/" in tok or tok.endswith(".sh") or tok.endswith(".py")
  317	
  318	
  319	def _bash_path_tokens(command: str) -> list:
  320	    """Extract path-shaped tokens from a Bash command string."""
  321	    import shlex
  322	    try:
  323	        tokens = shlex.split(command, posix=True)
  324	    except ValueError:
  325	        tokens = command.split()
  326	    return [tok for tok in tokens if _is_path_shaped(tok)]
  327	
  328	
  329	def _resolve_token(tok: str) -> list:
  330	    try:
  331	        resolved = os.path.realpath(tok)
  332	    except OSError:
  333	        resolved = os.path.abspath(tok)
  334	    return [resolved, os.path.abspath(tok)]
  335	
  336	
  337	def _match_any_prefix(prefixes: list, candidates: list):
  338	    for p in prefixes:
  339	        if isinstance(p, str) and _prefix_matches_any_candidate(p, candidates):
  340	            return p
  341	    return None
  342	
  343	
  344	def _gaode_bash_path_match(command):
  345	    if not command or not isinstance(command, str):
  346	        return None
  347	    prefixes = (load_policy() or {}).get("gaode_denied_bash_resolved_path_prefixes", []) or []
  348	    if not prefixes:
  349	        return None
  350	    for tok in _bash_path_tokens(command):
  351	        hit = _match_any_prefix(prefixes, _resolve_token(tok))
  352	        if hit:
  353	            return hit
  354	    return None
  355	
  356	
  357	def _gaode_host_glob_match(host_lower: str, text_lower: str) -> bool:
  358	    base = host_lower.replace("*.", "").replace("*", "")
  359	    return bool(base and base in text_lower)
  360	
  361	
  362	def _gaode_substring_signal(text_lower: str):
  363	    if "gaode" not in text_lower:
  364	        return None
  365	    if "://" in text_lower or "curl" in text_lower or "wget" in text_lower:
  366	        return "substring:gaode"
  367	    return None
  368	
  369	
  370	def _host_match_one(h_lower: str, text_lower: str) -> bool:
  371	    if "*" in h_lower:
  372	        return _gaode_host_glob_match(h_lower, text_lower)
  373	    return h_lower in text_lower
  374	
  375	
  376	def _gaode_network_match(command_or_url):
  377	    if not command_or_url or not isinstance(command_or_url, str):
  378	        return None
  379	    hosts = (load_policy() or {}).get("gaode_denied_network_hosts", []) or []
  380	    text_lower = command_or_url.lower()
  381	    for h in hosts:
  382	        if isinstance(h, str) and _host_match_one(h.lower(), text_lower):
  383	            return h
  384	    return _gaode_substring_signal(text_lower)
  385	
  386	
  387	def _env_var_regex(var: str) -> str:
  388	    import re
  389	    if var.endswith("*"):
  390	        stem = var[:-1]
  391	        return r"(?:\$\{?|\bexport\s+|\b)" + re.escape(stem) + r"[A-Z0-9_]*"
  392	    return r"(?:\$\{?|\bexport\s+|\b)" + re.escape(var) + r"\b"
  393	
  394	
  395	def _gaode_env_var_match(command):
  396	    if not command or not isinstance(command, str):
  397	        return None
  398	    import re
  399	    env_vars = (load_policy() or {}).get("gaode_denied_env_vars", []) or []
  400	    for v in env_vars:
  401	        if isinstance(v, str) and re.search(_env_var_regex(v), command):
  402	            return v
  403	    return None
  404	
  405	
  406	def _gaode_read_path_match(target):
  407	    if not target or not isinstance(target, str):
  408	        return None
  409	    prefixes = (load_policy() or {}).get("gaode_denied_read_path_prefixes", []) or []
  410	    return _match_any_prefix(prefixes, _candidate_targets(target))
  411	
  412	
  413	_GAODE_SURFACE_DISPATCH = {
  414	    "skill": _gaode_skill_match,
  415	    "bash-token": _gaode_bash_token_match,
  416	    "bash-resolved-path": _gaode_bash_path_match,
  417	    "network-host": _gaode_network_match,
  418	    "env-var": _gaode_env_var_match,
  419	    "read-path": _gaode_read_path_match,
  420	}
  421	
  422	
  423	def _gaode_decide_unknown(surface: str, matched_pattern: str) -> Tuple[bool, str]:
  424	    if _gaode_fail_closed():
  425	        return (False, f"gaode-policy: identity-default-deny on surface {surface} (matched {matched_pattern})")
  426	    return (True, "gaode-policy: identity-fail-open-disabled")
  427	
  428	
  429	def _gaode_decide(matched_pattern: str, role, surface: str) -> Tuple[bool, str]:
  430	    canonical_role = normalize_gaode_agent_id(role)
  431	    allowlist = _gaode_allowlist()
  432	    if not canonical_role:
  433	        return _gaode_decide_unknown(surface, matched_pattern)
  434	    if canonical_role in allowlist:
  435	        return (True, f"gaode-policy: role {canonical_role} in allowlist")
  436	    al = ", ".join(allowlist)
  437	    return (False, f"gaode-policy: role {canonical_role} not in allowlist {{{al}}} on surface {surface} (matched {matched_pattern})")
  438	
  439	
  440	def is_gaode_allowed(role, surface: str, target) -> Tuple[bool, str]:
  441	    """Return (allowed, reason) for a (role, surface, target) triple."""
  442	    try:
  443	        matcher = _GAODE_SURFACE_DISPATCH.get(surface)
  444	        if matcher is None:
  445	            return (True, "gaode-policy: unknown-surface")
  446	        matched_pattern = matcher(target)
  447	        if not matched_pattern:
  448	            return (True, "gaode-policy: not-a-gaode-target")
  449	        return _gaode_decide(matched_pattern, role, surface)
  450	    except Exception as e:  # pragma: no cover
  451	        sys.stderr.write(f"policy_registry.is_gaode_allowed: unexpected ({e})\n")
  452	        return (False, "gaode-policy: fail-closed-exception")
  453	
  454	
  455	def gaode_match_pattern(surface: str, target):
  456	    """Helper for the hook to retrieve the matched pattern for telemetry."""
  457	    matcher = _GAODE_SURFACE_DISPATCH.get(surface)
  458	    return None if matcher is None else matcher(target)
  459	
  460	
  461	def is_allowed(
  462	    role: str, tool_name: str, target_path: Optional[str]
  463	) -> Tuple[bool, str]:
  464	    """Return (allowed: bool, reason: str). See module docstring."""
  465	    try:
  466	        policy = load_policy()
  467	        if not policy:
  468	            return _fail_open_or_closed(role, "policy-unavailable")
  469	        role_pol = get_role_policy(role)
  470	        if role_pol.get("_unknown_role"):
  471	            return (False, f"unknown role {role}")
  472	        tool_check = _check_tool_lists(role_pol, tool_name)
  473	        if tool_check is not None:
  474	            return tool_check
  475	        if tool_name in WRITE_TOOLS:
  476	            return _check_write_path(role_pol, target_path)
  477	        return _check_read_path(role_pol, target_path)
  478	    except Exception as e:  # pragma: no cover
  479	        sys.stderr.write(f"policy_registry: unexpected ({e})\n")
  480	        return _fail_open_or_closed(role, "exception")

--- /root/.claude/hooks/pretool-tool-policy.py ---
exists True real /dev/shm/dev-workspace/dot-claude/hooks/pretool-tool-policy.py
    1	#!/usr/bin/env python3
    2	"""PreToolUse:* hook — enforce tool-policy.v1.json for all subagents.
    3	
    4	Single hook that consumes /root/.claude/policies/tool-policy.v1.json via
    5	lib.policy_registry.is_allowed() and lib.agent_resolver.resolve_agent_type().
    6	
    7	Behavior:
    8	  - Main agent (no agent_id, no subagent_type) -> exit 0 (orchestrator
    9	    gate handles main agent).
   10	  - Subagent role unresolvable -> exit 0 (downstream
   11	    pretool-subagent-code-block.py shim still applies as backstop).
   12	  - Resolved role + denied tool/path -> exit 2 with structured stderr
   13	    JSON: {"role", "tool", "target", "deny_reason"}.
   14	
   15	Bash policy bypass fix (T2.1):
   16	  - For Bash tool, parse the command with lib.bash_write_targets to
   17	    extract every shell write target (heredoc-stripped). Each extracted
   18	    path is authorized as if it were a Write target. The bash command
   19	    itself is also authorized for tool-list membership (target=None).
   20	  - This closes the heredoc/redirect bypass where a subagent could
   21	    write to a protected path via 'cat > FILE << EOF' or similar.
   22	
   23	Fail-safe: any unexpected exception logs to stderr and exits 0 to
   24	avoid bricking the tool pipeline on a hook bug.
   25	"""
   26	
   27	from __future__ import annotations
   28	
   29	import json
   30	import os
   31	import sys
   32	
   33	sys.path.insert(0, os.path.dirname(os.path.abspath(__file__)))
   34	
   35	from lib.agent_resolver import resolve_agent_type  # noqa: E402
   36	from lib.bash_write_targets import extract_bash_write_paths  # noqa: E402
   37	from lib.policy_registry import (  # noqa: E402
   38	    WRITE_TOOLS,
   39	    gaode_match_pattern,
   40	    is_allowed,
   41	    is_gaode_allowed,
   42	)
   43	
   44	GAODE_BASH_SURFACES = ("bash-token", "bash-resolved-path", "network-host", "env-var")
   45	GAODE_READ_TOOLS = {"Read", "Grep", "Glob"}
   46	
   47	
   48	def _read_payload() -> dict:
   49	    try:
   50	        return json.load(sys.stdin)
   51	    except json.JSONDecodeError:
   52	        return {}
   53	
   54	
   55	def _extract_targets(tool_name: str, tool_input: dict) -> list:
   56	    """Return list of policy targets for the (tool, input) pair.
   57	
   58	    For Bash: returns [None] followed by every extracted write target.
   59	    The leading None preserves tool-list authorization (allowed_tools /
   60	    denied_tools) even when the bash command has no write targets
   61	    (e.g. 'echo hello'). Each subsequent extracted path is then
   62	    authorized as a Write target — closing the heredoc/redirect bypass
   63	    where a subagent could write to a protected path via 'cat > FILE
   64	    << EOF' or other shell write idioms.
   65	    """
   66	    if not isinstance(tool_input, dict):
   67	        return [None]
   68	    if tool_name in WRITE_TOOLS:
   69	        return [tool_input.get("file_path") or tool_input.get("notebook_path")]
   70	    if tool_name == "Read":
   71	        return [tool_input.get("file_path")]
   72	    if tool_name == "Bash":
   73	        command = tool_input.get("command") or ""
   74	        return [None] + extract_bash_write_paths(command)
   75	    return [None]
   76	
   77	
   78	def _emit_block(role: str, tool: str, target, reason: str) -> None:
   79	    payload = {
   80	        "role": role,
   81	        "tool": tool,
   82	        "target": target,
   83	        "deny_reason": reason,
   84	    }
   85	    sys.stderr.write(
   86	        f"BLOCKED by tool-policy.v1: {json.dumps(payload, separators=(',', ':'))}\n"
   87	    )
   88	
   89	
   90	def _emit_gaode_block(role, tool, surface: str, target, reason: str) -> None:
   91	    """Emit a gaode-policy block with surface + matched_pattern fields."""
   92	    matched = gaode_match_pattern(surface, target)
   93	    payload = {
   94	        "role": role,
   95	        "tool": tool,
   96	        "surface": surface,
   97	        "matched_pattern": matched,
   98	        "deny_reason": reason,
   99	    }
  100	    sys.stderr.write(
  101	        f"BLOCKED by tool-policy.v1: {json.dumps(payload, separators=(',', ':'))}\n"
  102	    )
  103	
  104	
  105	def _check_gaode_skill(role, tool_name: str, tool_input: dict) -> None:
  106	    if tool_name != "Skill":
  107	        return
  108	    skill = tool_input.get("skill") or ""
  109	    allowed, reason = is_gaode_allowed(role, "skill", skill)
  110	    if not allowed:
  111	        _emit_gaode_block(role, tool_name, "skill", skill, reason)
  112	        sys.exit(2)
  113	
  114	
  115	def _check_gaode_bash(role, command: str) -> None:
  116	    """Run all four bash-related gaode matchers against the raw command string."""
  117	    if not command:
  118	        return
  119	    for surface in GAODE_BASH_SURFACES:
  120	        allowed, reason = is_gaode_allowed(role, surface, command)
  121	        if not allowed:
  122	            _emit_gaode_block(role, "Bash", surface, command, reason)
  123	            sys.exit(2)
  124	
  125	
  126	def _check_gaode_webfetch(role, tool_name: str, tool_input: dict) -> None:
  127	    if tool_name != "WebFetch":
  128	        return
  129	    url = tool_input.get("url") or ""
  130	    allowed, reason = is_gaode_allowed(role, "network-host", url)
  131	    if not allowed:
  132	        _emit_gaode_block(role, tool_name, "network-host", url, reason)
  133	        sys.exit(2)
  134	
  135	
  136	def _check_gaode_read(role, tool_name: str, tool_input: dict) -> None:
  137	    if tool_name not in GAODE_READ_TOOLS:
  138	        return
  139	    target = tool_input.get("file_path") or tool_input.get("path") or ""
  140	    allowed, reason = is_gaode_allowed(role, "read-path", target)
  141	    if not allowed:
  142	        _emit_gaode_block(role, tool_name, "read-path", target, reason)
  143	        sys.exit(2)
  144	
  145	
  146	def _gaode_pre_check(role, tool_name: str, tool_input: dict) -> None:
  147	    """Run all six gaode surface matchers BEFORE the standard tool/path check.
  148	
  149	    Exits 2 on first deny via _emit_gaode_block. Returns None when no surface
  150	    is hit OR when the role is in the gaode allowlist.
  151	    """
  152	    _check_gaode_skill(role, tool_name, tool_input)
  153	    if tool_name == "Bash":
  154	        _check_gaode_bash(role, tool_input.get("command") or "")
  155	    _check_gaode_webfetch(role, tool_name, tool_input)
  156	    _check_gaode_read(role, tool_name, tool_input)
  157	
  158	
  159	def _check_targets(role: str, tool_name: str, targets: list) -> None:
  160	    """Iterate targets, exit 2 on first deny. For Bash, treat each
  161	    extracted write target (idx > 0) as a Write authorization request."""
  162	    for idx, target in enumerate(targets):
  163	        check_tool = tool_name
  164	        if tool_name == "Bash" and idx > 0:
  165	            check_tool = "Write"
  166	        allowed, reason = is_allowed(role, check_tool, target)
  167	        if not allowed:
  168	            _emit_block(role, check_tool, target, reason)
  169	            sys.exit(2)
  170	
  171	
  172	def _gaode_identity_pre_check(role, tool_name: str, tool_input: dict) -> None:
  173	    """When agent_id is unresolvable, still enforce gaode default-deny.
  174	
  175	    Mirrors _gaode_pre_check but is called before the role==None early-exit so
  176	    identity-default-deny binds to gaode surfaces. Non-gaode surfaces remain
  177	    fail-open (legacy LOW-10) per the asymmetric policy in is_gaode_allowed.
  178	    """
  179	    _gaode_pre_check(None, tool_name, tool_input)
  180	
  181	
  182	def main() -> None:
  183	    data = _read_payload()
  184	    if not data:
  185	        sys.exit(0)
  186	    role = resolve_agent_type(data)
  187	    tool_name = data.get("tool_name")
  188	    if not isinstance(tool_name, str):
  189	        sys.exit(0)
  190	    tool_input = data.get("tool_input") or {}
  191	    if not role:
  192	        # Identity-unresolved: enforce gaode-only default-deny, then exit 0.
  193	        _gaode_identity_pre_check(role, tool_name, tool_input)
  194	        sys.exit(0)
  195	    # Gaode pre-check stage runs BEFORE standard tool/path enforcement so the
  196	    # deny reason is gaode-policy-shaped (surface + matched_pattern) instead of
  197	    # the generic write-target message.
  198	    _gaode_pre_check(role, tool_name, tool_input)
  199	    targets = _extract_targets(tool_name, tool_input)
  200	    _check_targets(role, tool_name, targets)
  201	    sys.exit(0)
  202	
  203	
  204	if __name__ == "__main__":
  205	    try:
  206	        main()
  207	    except Exception as e:  # pragma: no cover
  208	        sys.stderr.write(f"pretool-tool-policy: unexpected ({e})\n")
  209	        sys.exit(0)

 succeeded in 0ms:
    1	#!/usr/bin/env python3
    2	"""Resolve subagent identity to agent_type string.
    3	
    4	Refactored from pretool-subagent-code-block.py::_find_agent_type so that
    5	multiple hooks (pretool-subagent-code-block.py, pretool-tool-policy.py)
    6	can share one canonical lookup.
    7	
    8	Resolution order (first hit wins):
    9	  1. payload.subagent_type — if the runtime already injects the role label.
   10	  2. payload.agent_id matched against /spec cp-state files.
   11	  3. payload.agent_id matched against /dev dev-registry agent-index.json.
   12	  4. None — caller decides (main agent or unknown).
   13	
   14	Fail-safe: every I/O path is wrapped; on any unexpected exception we
   15	return None rather than raise.
   16	
   17	LOW-10 (2026-05-07): callers MUST NOT hard-block on `None`. Claude Code
   18	may dispatch subagents before any sentinel-creating tool runs, so the
   19	agent-index entry can legitimately be missing during the first write.
   20	The canonical contract is: hard-block on resolved-but-wrong role; for
   21	None, degrade to warn + allow. See pretool-subagent-code-block.py.
   22	
   23	GAODE-SCOPED EXCEPTION (spec-20260508-221237 M1, 2026-05-09): the LOW-10
   24	fail-open is preserved for non-gaode tool calls. For tool calls that hit
   25	any of the six gaode surfaces (skill / bash-token / bash-resolved-path /
   26	network-host / env-var / read-path), the gaode sub-policy in
   27	policy_registry.is_gaode_allowed enforces fail-CLOSED on unresolved
   28	agent_id (gaode_fail_closed_on_unknown_agent: true). Rationale: gaode
   29	calls are low-frequency and a one-cycle false deny is recoverable; a
   30	false allow silently breaks the spec authority chain that every later
   31	milestone (M2 schema, M3 web app, M4 exporters) depends on.
   32	"""
   33	
   34	from __future__ import annotations
   35	
   36	import glob
   37	import json
   38	import os
   39	from typing import Optional
   40	
   41	
   42	def _read_json(path: str) -> Optional[dict]:
   43	    try:
   44	        with open(path) as f:
   45	            return json.load(f)
   46	    except (OSError, json.JSONDecodeError):
   47	        return None
   48	
   49	
   50	def _match_cp_state(path: str, agent_id: str) -> Optional[str]:
   51	    data = _read_json(path)
   52	    if not data or data.get("agent_id") != agent_id:
   53	        return None
   54	    t = data.get("agent_type")
   55	    return t if isinstance(t, str) else None
   56	
   57	
   58	def _read_match(path: str, agent_id: str) -> Optional[dict]:
   59	    """Return cp-state dict if agent_id matches, else None.
   60	
   61	    Unlike _match_cp_state (which returns just agent_type), this returns
   62	    the full payload so disambiguation in _scan_cp_state_files can inspect
   63	    is_running and checked_in_at without re-reading the file.
   64	    """
   65	    data = _read_json(path)
   66	    if not data or data.get("agent_id") != agent_id:
   67	        return None
   68	    t = data.get("agent_type")
   69	    if not isinstance(t, str):
   70	        return None
   71	    return data
   72	
   73	
   74	def _pick_active(matches: list) -> Optional[str]:
   75	    """Disambiguate among is_running=true matches.
   76	
   77	    Cross-role active collision -> None (fail closed).
   78	    Same-role active collision -> that agent_type.
   79	    Single active -> its agent_type.
   80	    """
   81	    types = {m.get("agent_type") for m in matches}
   82	    if len(types) > 1:
   83	        return None  # F14 M8: cross-role active collision -> fail closed
   84	    return next(iter(types))  # F14 M9: deterministic same-role active
   85	
   86	
   87	def _lookup_dev_registry_index(agent_id: str, project_dir: str) -> Optional[str]:
   88	    index_path = f"{project_dir}/.claude/dev-registry/agent-index.json"
   89	    data = _read_json(index_path)
   90	    if not data:
   91	        return None
   92	    value = data.get(agent_id)
   93	    return value if isinstance(value, str) else None
   94	
   95	
   96	_FAIL_CLOSED = object()  # sentinel: cross-role active collision -> deny
   97	
   98	
   99	def _glob_cp_state(project_dir: str) -> list:
  100	    pattern = f"{project_dir}/.claude/specs/*/cp-state-*.json"
  101	    try:
  102	        return glob.glob(pattern)
  103	    except OSError:
  104	        return []
  105	
  106	
  107	def _scan_cp_state_files(agent_id: str, project_dir: str):
  108	    """Tri-state cp-state scan.
  109	
  110	    Returns: agent_type str | _FAIL_CLOSED sentinel | None.
  111	      - str: a single resolved active match (F14 M9)
  112	      - _FAIL_CLOSED: active cross-role collision (F14 M8 fail-closed);
  113	        caller MUST NOT fall through to agent-index.
  114	      - None: no match or inactive-only (AC-3 non-authoritative); caller
  115	        MAY fall through to agent-index.
  116	    """
  117	    paths = _glob_cp_state(project_dir)
  118	    matches = [d for d in (_read_match(p, agent_id) for p in paths) if d]
  119	    if not matches:
  120	        return None
  121	    active = [m for m in matches if m.get("is_running")]
  122	    if not active:
  123	        return None
  124	    picked = _pick_active(active)
  125	    return _FAIL_CLOSED if picked is None else picked
  126	
  127	
  128	def _resolve_by_id(agent_id: str) -> Optional[str]:
  129	    project_dir = os.environ.get("CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR", os.getcwd())
  130	    spec_hit = _scan_cp_state_files(agent_id, project_dir)
  131	    if spec_hit is _FAIL_CLOSED:
  132	        return None  # active collision: deny, do NOT consult agent-index
  133	    if isinstance(spec_hit, str):
  134	        return spec_hit
  135	    return _lookup_dev_registry_index(agent_id, project_dir)
  136	
  137	
  138	def resolve_agent_type(payload: dict) -> Optional[str]:
  139	    """Public API: PreToolUse stdin payload -> agent_type or None."""
  140	    if not isinstance(payload, dict):
  141	        return None
  142	    direct = payload.get("subagent_type")
  143	    if isinstance(direct, str) and direct:
  144	        return direct
  145	    agent_id = payload.get("agent_id")
  146	    if not agent_id or not isinstance(agent_id, str):
  147	        return None
  148	    return _resolve_by_id(agent_id)

 succeeded in 0ms:
    1	#!/usr/bin/env python3
    2	"""Tool-policy registry loader and authorization decisions.
    3	
    4	Reads /root/.claude/policies/tool-policy.v1.json and provides a single
    5	is_allowed(role, tool_name, target_path) entrypoint shared by:
    6	  - pretool-tool-policy.py (canonical enforcement)
    7	  - pretool-subagent-code-block.py (backstop shim)
    8	
    9	Fail-safe contract:
   10	  - On policy load failure (file missing, parse error), `dev` role gets
   11	    fail-safe ALLOW (preserves the historical ALLOWED_TYPES = {'dev'}
   12	    behavior), every other role gets fail-CLOSED DENY.
   13	  - On any unexpected exception, return (True, "fail-safe-exception")
   14	    for the dev role, (False, "fail-closed-exception") for others.
   15	
   16	Path prefix matching (T1.2 fix for B.11):
   17	  - Targets are normalized via os.path.realpath (collapses symlinks
   18	    such as /root/.claude -> /dev/shm/dev-workspace/dot-claude/).
   19	  - Universal allow: prefix '*' always matches.
   20	  - Glob: prefix containing '*' uses fnmatch.fnmatchcase, with a
   21	    trailing '*' auto-appended so '*/docs/dev/foo-' matches any suffix
   22	    after the prefix anchor (e.g. inside any worktree).
   23	  - Absolute anchor (starts with '/'): interpreted as project-root
   24	    anchored, not as a raw substring. For example '/.claude/hooks/'
   25	    becomes '<CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR>/.claude/hooks/' (plus its realpath
   26	    form) and matches only that path or descendants. This closes the old
   27	    substring-collision bug where an anchor could match merely because
   28	    the same text appeared somewhere inside an unrelated target path.
   29	  - Relative prefix (doesn't start with '/' and contains no '*'):
   30	    falls back to legacy substring containment. New policies should
   31	    always anchor with '/' or '*'; relative prefixes remain only for
   32	    backward compatibility.
   33	"""
   34	
   35	from __future__ import annotations
   36	
   37	import fnmatch
   38	import json
   39	import os
   40	import sys
   41	from typing import Optional, Tuple
   42	
   43	POLICY_PATH = "/root/.claude/policies/tool-policy.v1.json"
   44	WRITE_TOOLS = {"Write", "Edit", "NotebookEdit", "MultiEdit"}
   45	ALLOWED_TYPES_FALLBACK = {"dev"}
   46	
   47	_CACHE: Optional[dict] = None
   48	_CACHE_LOADED = False
   49	
   50	
   51	def load_policy() -> Optional[dict]:
   52	    """Read tool-policy.v1.json. Returns dict or None on error."""
   53	    global _CACHE, _CACHE_LOADED
   54	    if _CACHE_LOADED:
   55	        return _CACHE
   56	    _CACHE_LOADED = True
   57	    try:
   58	        with open(POLICY_PATH) as f:
   59	            _CACHE = json.load(f)
   60	    except (OSError, json.JSONDecodeError) as e:
   61	        sys.stderr.write(f"policy_registry: load failed ({e})\n")
   62	        _CACHE = None
   63	    return _CACHE
   64	
   65	
   66	def _reset_cache_for_tests() -> None:
   67	    global _CACHE, _CACHE_LOADED
   68	    _CACHE = None
   69	    _CACHE_LOADED = False
   70	
   71	
   72	def get_role_policy(role: str) -> dict:
   73	    """Return the role's policy dict, or a deny-all fallback."""
   74	    policy = load_policy()
   75	    if not policy:
   76	        return {"allowed_tools": [], "default_action": "deny"}
   77	    roles = policy.get("roles", {})
   78	    return roles.get(
   79	        role,
   80	        {"allowed_tools": [], "default_action": "deny", "_unknown_role": True},
   81	    )
   82	
   83	
   84	def _entry_matches(entry: str, tool_name: str) -> bool:
   85	    if entry.endswith("*"):
   86	        return tool_name.startswith(entry[:-1])
   87	    return entry == tool_name
   88	
   89	
   90	def _tool_in_list(tool_name: str, items: list) -> bool:
   91	    """Match tool against a list, supporting `*` wildcard suffix."""
   92	    for entry in items or []:
   93	        if isinstance(entry, str) and _entry_matches(entry, tool_name):
   94	            return True
   95	    return False
   96	
   97	
   98	def _normalize_target(target: str) -> str:
   99	    """Resolve target to canonical absolute path, collapsing symlinks.
  100	
  101	    On any failure (target missing, permission denied), fall back to
  102	    os.path.abspath so policy decisions never crash on non-existent paths
  103	    (e.g. Write to a file that does not exist yet).
  104	    """
  105	    try:
  106	        return os.path.realpath(target)
  107	    except OSError:
  108	        try:
  109	            return os.path.abspath(target)
  110	        except Exception:  # pragma: no cover
  111	            return target
  112	
  113	
  114	def _glob_match(prefix: str, target_canonical: str) -> bool:
  115	    """fnmatch glob match; conditionally auto-extend trailing '*'.
  116	
  117	    Auto-append '*' ONLY when the pattern ends with a prefix marker
  118	    (a trailing '-' for artifact prefixes like '*/docs/dev/qa-report-',
  119	    or a trailing '/' for directory prefixes like '*/docs/dev/overnight/').
  120	    Patterns that end with anything else (e.g., a literal filename like
  121	    '*/cp-state-ba.json') are matched EXACTLY as written, with no
  122	    auto-suffix. This closes the codex-flagged leak where
  123	    '*/cp-state-ba.json' would otherwise match
  124	    '/root/.../cp-state-ba.json.bak' or '/root/.../cp-state-ba.json/evil'.
  125	
  126	    Patterns already ending in '*' are passed through untouched.
  127	    """
  128	    if prefix.endswith("*") or prefix.endswith("-") or prefix.endswith("/"):
  129	        pattern = prefix if prefix.endswith("*") else prefix + "*"
  130	    else:
  131	        pattern = prefix
  132	    return fnmatch.fnmatchcase(target_canonical, pattern)
  133	
  134	
  135	def _project_dir() -> str:
  136	    """Return the logical Claude project dir used for root-anchored policy."""
  137	    return os.path.abspath(os.environ.get("CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR", "/root"))

codex
Codex feedback: I would not sign off on the BA ticket as written. Findings (1) and (2) are valid blockers; (3) is partly right but needs reframing.

## 1. Policy Write/Edit/MultiEdit for timeline/transportation

Valid security regression.

- Ticket grants `Write`, `Edit`, `MultiEdit` in policy: `docs/dev/ticket-20260509-114002.md:331-332`, `350-351`.
- Actual agents only declare `Read`, `Bash`, `Skill`: `.claude/agents/timeline.md:7-10`, `.claude/agents/transportation.md:10-13`.
- Both prompts explicitly forbid Write/Edit due the Feb. 13 data-loss incident: `.claude/agents/timeline.md:21-35`, `.claude/agents/transportation.md:22-35`.

The prompt prohibition is not an enforcement boundary. With the proposed role, `policy_registry.is_allowed()` allows direct writes to `data/.../timeline.json` because `Write/Edit/MultiEdit` are in `allowed_tools` and `*/data/` is in `allowed_write_path_prefixes`.

Also, the ownership hook does not save this:
- It only handles `Write|Edit`, not `MultiEdit`.
- It allows writes to owned files; it does not enforce “Write tool forbidden.”

Required BA fix: policy roles should not authorize `Write`, `Edit`, or `MultiEdit` for these agents. Prefer explicit `denied_tools: ["Write", "Edit", "MultiEdit", "NotebookEdit"]`; keep persistence through `Bash` + `scripts/save.py`.

Add AC: timeline/transportation direct `Write`, `Edit`, and `MultiEdit` to their own `data/.../*.json` must exit nonzero.

## 2. Shape-based dispatch and documentation blackhole

Valid. BA has moved the false-positive risk from bash-token scanning to read-path scanning.

The inconsistency is internal:

- Decision 1 says path-shaped leaves, including `Edit.old_string-with-path`, are fed to read-path matcher: `docs/dev/ticket-20260509-114002.md:316-320`.
- AC12 requires blocking an embedded banned skill path inside `Edit.old_string`: `docs/dev/ticket-20260509-114002.md:445-450`.
- AC15 only protects docs containing free text without a slash/path: `docs/dev/ticket-20260509-114002.md:463-468`.

So either:
- scanner treats the whole leaf as one path → AC12 will not actually block `old_string:"path = '/root/.../skill.md'"`; or
- scanner extracts embedded path substrings → documentation edits containing that path will be blocked.

Required BA fix: do not scan content fields like `old_string` / `new_string` for read-path. Scan only fields semantically used as paths/globs/filenames. Add AC15 case where a BA-editable docs file contains `see /root/.claude/skills/<banned-maps>/skill.md` in `old_string`; expected result must not be a gaode-policy denial.

## 3. AC9 end-to-end positive tests

Partly agree, but not for the exact read-path reason.

Current code’s read-path standard fallthrough only checks `denied_path_prefixes`; it does not enforce `allowed_path_prefixes`: `/root/.claude/hooks/lib/policy_registry.py:247-251`. The proposed timeline/transportation roles also do not define read denies. So a `Read` of the banned skill path by timeline would pass standard policy after the gaode allowlist pre-check.

However, AC9/T2 is still under-specified and can fail end-to-end:

- AC9 says all six surfaces exit 0 for timeline/transportation: `docs/dev/ticket-20260509-114002.md:426-431`.
- Decision 3 says T2 covers allowlist roles across every gaode-relevant tool, including `WebFetch`, MCP tools, `NotebookEdit`, etc.: `docs/dev/ticket-20260509-114002.md:365-367`.
- Proposed roles do not allow `WebFetch`, MCP tools, or `NotebookEdit`, so those allowlist-positive cases will fail standard `allowed_tools` after gaode pre-check.

Required BA fix: split AC9 into:
1. gaode pre-check allowlist behavior for all six surfaces; and
2. full hook exit-0 only for tools actually allowed by the role, e.g. `Skill`, `Bash`, `Read`.

For network-host positive coverage, use allowed `Bash` fixtures, not MCP/WebFetch, unless the role intentionally grants those tools.

## Additional blocker

AC15 uses `subagent_type:"meals"` and expects `Read` to exit 0, but current policy has no `roles.meals`; cycle 3 only adds timeline/transportation. Current hook returns generic deny: `unknown role meals`.

So AC15 as written will fail unless BA either adds least-privilege roles for the data agents or changes the false-positive regression tests to use an existing policy role such as `ba`/`qa`.
